================================================================================
 TTW PAVEMENT STANDALONE: FULL WRITE-UP AND SOURCE CODE
 Use this as the blueprint for a new tool (e.g. TTW Retaining Walls)
================================================================================

Version 1.0.0. Repo: swiine/Detail_Grid. Last commit at the time of writing: 3b74b0a.

HOW TO USE THIS FILE
--------------------
Part 1 explains what the program does and how it is built, in plain English.
Part 2 is a step-by-step plan for building a retaining wall tool the same way.
Part 3 is every source file the standalone program needs, in full, in build
order. Each file starts with a "FILE: path" banner, so you can copy it back out.

If you give this file to Claude (or another AI) to start the retaining wall
program, say something like:
  "This is our pavement tool. Build a retaining wall tool with the same
   structure, brand, wizard, reports and export folders. Here are our retaining
   wall spreadsheets: <attach them>."


================================================================================
 PART 1: WHAT WE BUILT
================================================================================

1.1  THE TWO TOOLS
------------------
a) The Civil 3D 2026 plugin (src/PavementBuildup): commands TTWPVMT (menu),
   PAVEQUICK and TTWPVMTAI (AI settings only). It draws pavement build-up
   details straight into the drawing. NOT included in Part 3, apart from the
   two files the standalone shares (Brand.cs and PreviewPanel.cs).

b) The standalone Windows app, "TTW Pavement.exe" (src/PavementBuildup.Standalone).
   No Civil 3D needed. It has two tabs:
     - Detail: paste a build-up, see the preview, pick a scale (1:10 etc.)
       and a standard, then save a DXF.
     - Design: a 4-step wizard that does the company pavement calculations
       and writes an HTML + Excel report.

There is also a web page version (Blazor WebAssembly, src/PavementBuildup.Web)
for the Detail side. It is not included here.

1.2  RELEASE FOLDER LAYOUT (TTW_Pave_Tool V1.0.0)
-------------------------------------------------
  TTW_Pave_Tool V1.0.0\
    Civil3D Tool\                 <- the Autoloader bundle for Civil 3D
    _support\_Standards\
        TTW.json                  <- the company CAD standard (layers, text, dims)
        TTW_Pave_Temp.dwg         <- template drawing with all the layers
    Standalone Tool\
        TTW Pavement.exe
        TTW Pavement Standalone - Instructions.txt
        _Exports\                 <- everything the app saves goes here
            2026-10-14\<job number>\<pavement name>\  (files go in here)

build.ps1 (Part 3) makes this layout.

1.3  PROJECTS (how the code is split)
-------------------------------------
Each part sits in its own project, so the maths can be tested without Windows
or AutoCAD.

  PavementBuildup.Core       Plain C#, no Windows, no AutoCAD.
                             - Reading build-up text (SmartReader, BuildupParser)
                             - Layout maths for the detail (DetailLayout)
                             - The CAD standard (CadStandard, StandardStore)
                             - Materials and hatches (MaterialLibrary)
                             - DESIGN CALCULATIONS (Core/Design/*):
                                 FlexibleDesign  Austroads granular pavement + company table
                                 ConcreteDesign  T48 / T34 slab checks (wheels, posts, UDL,
                                                 punching shear, deflection)
                                 RoadDesign      Traffic -> DESA -> flexible design
                                 SlabDesign      Industrial slab, all checks
                                 CalcResult      Result object (lines, pass/fail, tables)
                                 CalcReport      HTML report
                                 Inputs          Reads "20t", "1e6", "5%", "150mm"
  PavementBuildup.Export     DXF writer (netDxf) and SVG preview.
  PavementBuildup.Reports    Excel report (ClosedXML).
  PavementBuildup.Standalone WinForms app: window, wizard, folders, settings.
  PavementBuildup            The Civil 3D plugin. The standalone borrows Brand.cs
                             and PreviewPanel.cs from it.
  tests/                     xUnit tests (215 passing). The design tests check
                             the code against numbers from the company workbooks.

1.4  KEY IDEAS WORTH COPYING
----------------------------
1. The CalcResult pattern (Core/Design/CalcResult.cs).
   Every calculation returns a CalcResult with:
     - Title, Reference (e.g. "Austroads AGPT02 Fig 8.4")
     - Lines: a list of (Label, Value, Unit, Reference, Kind). Kind is one of
       Input, Step, Result, Ok, Fail or Warning.
     - Headline: one sentence answer, e.g. "Use a 200 mm slab".
     - Pass: true, false or null.
     - Parts: sub-checks (each one its own CalcResult).
     - Tables: "what if" tables (title, columns, rows; a row starting with "►"
       is the current case).
     - Notes.
   Because everything is a CalcResult, ONE HTML report writer and ONE Excel
   writer work for every kind of calculation. For retaining walls, make the
   checks (sliding, overturning, bearing, stem bending...) return CalcResults
   and the reports work straight away.

2. Copy the spreadsheet maths, then prove it.
   We read the company .xls/.xlsx formulas (with NPOI), put the same formulas
   in C#, and wrote tests that check the C# against the workbook numbers. We
   even kept a workbook quirk ((1 - v^12)) so the answers match. Do the same
   for the wall sheets: one test per worked example in the sheet.

3. Reading values with units (Core/Design/Inputs.cs).
   Engineers can type "20t", "1 x 10^6", "5%" or "150mm". TryRead gives back
   the value or a plain error message. Use it for wall heights, surcharges,
   soil angles and so on.

4. The 4-step wizard (Standalone/DesignWizard.cs).
     Step 1 Project: project, job number, design name, engineer, checker, type.
     Step 2 Ground:  CBR quick-pick buttons, soil depth, Es.
     Step 3 Loads:   traffic (known or from counts) or slab loads.
     Step 4 Result:  answer, all checks, what-if tables, preview, buttons
                     (Draw detail / Report / Copy / Save design / Start again).
   It has a live answer line while you type, checks the inputs before Next,
   and saves and opens .ttwdesign files (JSON).
   For walls:
     Step 1 Project, step 2 Wall (type, height, geometry), step 3 Soil and
     loads (phi, gamma, c, surcharge, water, seismic), step 4 Result.

5. Reports (CalcReport.cs + ExcelReport.cs).
   The HTML and the Excel are saved side by side and link to each other. The
   HTML prints to PDF, with sign-off boxes for Designed / Checked / Approved.
   The Excel has a Summary sheet, a What-if sheet, one sheet per check and a
   Notes sheet. Numbers are saved as real numbers.

6. Export folders (Standalone/AppFolders.cs).
   Files are saved to _Exports\yyyy-MM-dd\<job>\<design name>\, with
   "No job number" / "Untitled" when blank. Empty folders are tidied away, and
   loose files are moved into their date folder at startup.

7. Brand (PavementBuildup/Brand.cs).
   Water Blue #024360, Alabaster #F7F4E7, Arial Nova (falls back to Arial).
   Header band with the logo, flat buttons, status line, DPI scaling (Brand.Px).
   Brand.Decorate(form, title, subtitle, status) styles any window.

8. The CAD standard (CadStandard + StandardStore + standards/TTW.json).
   Layers, text and dim styles, scales and notes are in a JSON file, not in
   the code. The default is _support\_Standards\TTW.json; the user can pick
   another. In Civil 3D, layers and styles are copied from TTW_Pave_Temp.dwg.
   A wall tool can use the SAME TTW.json (add wall layers to it) and its own
   template dwg.

9. Settings (StandaloneSettings.cs).
   Saved in %APPDATA%\PavementBuildup\standalone.json: standards folder,
   standard file and scale. Use a new folder name for the wall tool, e.g.
   %APPDATA%\TTWWalls\.

1.5  BUILDING
-------------
- .NET 8 SDK. The standalone targets net8.0 / win-x64 and uses
  FrameworkReference Microsoft.WindowsDesktop.App.WindowsForms, so it builds
  even on Linux CI.
- It is published as one file, framework-dependent (needs the .NET 8 Desktop
  Runtime, which comes with Civil 3D 2026):
    dotnet publish src/PavementBuildup.Standalone -c Release
- NuGet packages: netDxf 2023.11.10 (Export) and ClosedXML 0.104.2 (Reports).
- Tests: dotnet test tests/PavementBuildup.Core.Tests
- build.ps1 builds the whole release folder.

1.6  HISTORY (what was asked and done, in order)
------------------------------------------------
 1.  Reader improvements: runway build-ups, bullets, "asphalt concrete" seen as
     asphalt, membrane role labels kept.
 2.  Branded windows from TTW.BrandedWindow.zip (header band, logo, colours).
 3.  Autodesk rules: Application.ShowModalDialog and AutoCAD file dialogs;
     references Copy Local = False.
 4.  Button styling fixed.
 5.  One command, TTWPVMT, opens a menu of all tools. PAVEQUICK kept.
 6.  Bloat removed: only the TTWPVMT and PAVEQUICK commands, plus a simple
     "TTW_Pvmt - Instructions.txt".
 7.  Menu made wider; Back button added.
 8.  AI settings only through the TTWPVMTAI command.
 9.  Standalone tool: Windows app plus web page.
 10. Standalone preview fit fixed; standards folder picker; scale picker;
     titles wider than the detail wrap to 2 lines; folder button no longer
     hidden.
 11. Design calculations from the company workbooks:
       T48_and_T34_Industrial_Pavement_Design.xls
       240929_Pavement_build-ups_1_3.xlsx
 12. Design made easier, then turned into the 4-step wizard.
 13. Release folder structure TTW_Pave_Tool V1.0.0.
 14. Default standard is _support\_Standards\TTW.json (can be changed).
 15. Template TTW_Pave_Temp.dwg: layers and styles are copied in when drawing.
 16. Exports go into dated folders, then into job number / design name folders.
 17. Subgrade label notes the compaction and soil type
     ("SUBGRADE COMPACTED TO 98% SMDD AT ±2% OMC", editable).
 18. Report saved as HTML + Excel, linked to each other.

1.7  KNOWN OPEN ITEMS
---------------------
- The break block TTW_stdCountry_Block_Break is not in TTW_Pave_Temp.dwg.
- The dim style is TTW_stdGlobal_Dim_mm (an _M version also exists).
- Nothing has been run inside Civil 3D or on Windows by the AI. It was tested
  with unit tests and DXF/preview checks only. Test on a real PC before use.
- All results are a design aid. An engineer must check and sign them off.


================================================================================
 PART 2: PLAN FOR A RETAINING WALL TOOL (same pattern)
================================================================================

1. Gather what's needed:
   - the company retaining wall spreadsheets (.xls/.xlsx, with formulas)
   - the wall types you design (cantilever RC, block or segmental, gravity,
     crib, sleeper/post, gabion...)
   - which standards they follow (AS 4678 Earth-retaining structures, AS 3600,
     AS 4100, AS 1170.4 seismic, etc.)
   - a few finished jobs with their numbers, used as test cases
   - a template dwg with the wall layers.

2. Make new projects by copying the pavement ones:
     TTW.Walls.Core        (copy CalcResult, CalcReport, Inputs, CadStandard,
                            StandardStore; write the new calculations here)
     TTW.Walls.Export      (DXF of the wall section: copy the DxfExport pattern)
     TTW.Walls.Reports     (copy ExcelReport as is: it only uses CalcResult)
     TTW.Walls.Standalone  (copy Program, AppFolders, StandaloneSettings and
                            the DesignWizard layout; link Brand.cs)
     tests                 (one test per spreadsheet example)

3. Calculations to write (each returns a CalcResult, with each check as a Part):
     Earth pressure (Rankine / Coulomb Ka, Kp, K0), surcharge, water, seismic
     Sliding FoS, overturning FoS, bearing pressure / eccentricity (middle third)
     Global stability (usually stays in Slide/Geo5, so just note it)
     Stem / base bending and shear (AS 3600), reinforcement
     Post / sleeper walls: embedment (Broms or simplified), post bending
     What-if tables: wall height vs base width, phi vs FoS, and so on.

4. Wizard steps:
     1 Project   project, job number, wall name, engineer, checker, wall type
     2 Wall      height, toe and heel, stem thickness, backfill slope,
                 embedment
     3 Soil &    phi, gamma, c, bearing capacity, surcharge, water, seismic
       loads
     4 Result    pass/fail per check, governing FoS, what-if tables, wall
                 picture, Draw (DXF) / Report (HTML + Excel)

5. Exports: _Exports\yyyy-MM-dd\<job>\<wall name>\ (AppFolders.ForDesign as is).


================================================================================

================================================================================
Order: shared props, Core (models, reader, standard, layout), Core/Design (the
calculations), Export, Reports, the shared plugin files (Brand, PreviewPanel),
Standalone, build script, standard JSON, instructions, design tests.

<Project Sdk="Microsoft.NET.Sdk">
  <!-- Pure logic (models, parser, layout, presets). No AutoCAD references so it can be unit tested anywhere. -->
  <PropertyGroup>
    <TargetFramework>net8.0</TargetFramework>
    <RootNamespace>PavementBuildup.Core</RootNamespace>
  </PropertyGroup>
  <ItemGroup>
    <!-- Same text users upload to an AI chat; the plugin sends it with "Interpret with AI". -->
    <EmbeddedResource Include="..\..\AI Pavement Build-up Prompt.txt" LogicalName="AiPrompt.txt" />
  </ItemGroup>
</Project>


################################################################################
# FILE: src/PavementBuildup.Core/Models.cs   (106 lines)
################################################################################

namespace PavementBuildup.Core;

/// <summary>One course of the pavement, listed top (surface) to bottom.</summary>
public sealed class PavementLayer
{
    /// <summary>Material / course description, e.g. "SMA 10 SURFACE COURSE".</summary>
    public string Description { get; set; } = "";

    /// <summary>Thickness in millimetres (of each layer when <see cref="Lifts"/> &gt; 1). Zero means a membrane drawn as a line.</summary>
    public double ThicknessMm { get; set; }

    /// <summary>Number of equal layers ("2 x 150mm ... ROAD BASE"). Drawn as one course with a line between layers.</summary>
    public int Lifts { get; set; } = 1;

    /// <summary>Full depth of the course: thickness x layers.</summary>
    [System.Text.Json.Serialization.JsonIgnore]
    public double TotalMm => Math.Max(0, ThicknessMm) * Math.Max(1, Lifts);

    /// <summary>
    /// The layer name it was written with ("Surface Course" in "Surface Course: 50mm Marshall Asphalt"). Not drawn;
    /// only helps pick the hatch.
    /// </summary>
    public string Role { get; set; } = "";

    /// <summary>Hatch pattern name. Empty or "AUTO" picks one from the description; "NONE" leaves it unhatched.</summary>
    public string HatchPattern { get; set; } = MaterialLibrary.Auto;

    /// <summary>Multiplier on the automatic hatch scale for this layer.</summary>
    public double HatchScale { get; set; } = 1.0;

    /// <summary>Hatch angle in degrees. Null uses the material default.</summary>
    public double? HatchAngle { get; set; }

    /// <summary>Reinforcement mats in this course (at most one top and one bottom). Empty = unreinforced.</summary>
    public List<Reinforcement> Reinforcement { get; set; } = new();

    /// <summary>The reinforcement as short text ("H16@150 c50"), for the course table. Setting it parses the text.</summary>
    [System.Text.Json.Serialization.JsonIgnore]
    public string ReinforcementText
    {
        get => ReinforcementParser.Format(Reinforcement);
        set => Reinforcement = ReinforcementParser.Parse(value);
    }

    public PavementLayer Clone()
    {
        var c = (PavementLayer)MemberwiseClone();
        c.Reinforcement = (Reinforcement ?? new()).Select(r => r.Clone()).ToList();
        return c;
    }
}

/// <summary>A named pavement build-up (e.g. "TYPE A - CARRIAGEWAY").</summary>
public sealed class Buildup
{
    public string Name { get; set; } = "PAVEMENT BUILD-UP";
    public List<PavementLayer> Layers { get; set; } = new();

    public bool ShowSubgrade { get; set; } = true;
    public string SubgradeText { get; set; } = "SUBGRADE";

    public double TotalThicknessMm => Layers.Sum(l => l.TotalMm);

    public Buildup Clone() => new()
    {
        Name = Name,
        Layers = Layers.Select(l => l.Clone()).ToList(),
        ShowSubgrade = ShowSubgrade,
        SubgradeText = SubgradeText,
    };
}

/// <summary>Per-drawing choices. Layers, hatches, text and wording come from the <see cref="CadStandard"/>.</summary>
public sealed class DetailSettings
{
    /// <summary>Width of the section strip in real-world millimetres.</summary>
    public double WidthMm { get; set; } = 1000;

    /// <summary>Detail scale denominator, i.e. 10 for 1:10. Sizes text, dimensions and hatching.</summary>
    public double ScaleDenominator { get; set; } = 10;

    /// <summary>Global multiplier applied on top of every layer's hatch scale.</summary>
    public double HatchScaleMultiplier { get; set; } = 1.0;

    /// <summary>Subgrade strip depth in real-world millimetres.</summary>
    public double SubgradeDepthMm { get; set; } = 150;

    /// <summary>
    /// Drawing units for this drawing: "STANDARD" (use the CAD standard's units), "AUTO" (read INSUNITS), MM, CM or M.
    /// Build-up thicknesses are always typed in millimetres; this only sets how big 1 mm is in the drawing.
    /// </summary>
    public string UnitsOverride { get; set; } = DetailLayout.UseStandardUnits;

    public bool CreateBlock { get; set; } = true;
    public bool ShowDimensions { get; set; } = true;
    public bool ShowBreakLines { get; set; } = true;
    public bool ShowTitle { get; set; } = true;

    /// <summary>
    /// CAD standard file (layers, hatches, styles). Blank uses the personal standard in
    /// %APPDATA%\PavementBuildup\standard.json; point it at a shared drive for a company standard.
    /// </summary>
    public string StandardPath { get; set; } = "";

    public DetailSettings Clone() => (DetailSettings)MemberwiseClone();
}


################################################################################
# FILE: src/PavementBuildup.Core/CadStandard.cs   (437 lines)
